#5222. American Bitcoin Says Q3 Net Income Reached $126M as BTC Reserve Grew

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: American Bitcoin reported approximately $126 million in third-quarter net income and said its Bitcoin reserve increased through mining rather than new spot purchases during the period. The November 4 release also cites about $221 million in adjusted EBITDA and an approximately 5% increase in satoshis per share.

American Bitcoin reported approximately $126 million in third-quarter net income and said its Bitcoin reserve increased through mining rather than new spot purchases during the period. The November 4 release also cites about $221 million in adjusted EBITDA and an approximately 5% increase in satoshis per share. Those figures put the focus on an increasingly important distinction among public Bitcoin businesses: the difference between growing a treasury and growing the amount of Bitcoin attributable to each share.

The company says it did not issue shares through its at-the-market program or purchase Bitcoin during the quarter. Instead, management credits mining operations with reserve growth despite seasonal electricity cost pressure. It also highlights a roughly 5% increase in sats per share. This metric is worth checking because a rising total Bitcoin balance can be less impressive if the share count rises even faster. Yet sats per share is not an accounting profit measure or a guarantee of shareholder return. Investors also need to consider debt, other assets and liabilities, operating expenses and the market price paid for the stock.

American Bitcoin reported net income and adjusted EBITDA of different magnitudes. Adjusted EBITDA can help explain management's view of operating performance, but exclusions and accounting policies matter. In mining and treasury companies, the price of Bitcoin, the valuation of held assets, depreciation of mining equipment and financing arrangements can all materially affect results. A single strong quarter should therefore be read beside the company's complete financial statements and reconciliation of non-GAAP figures. Comparing adjusted EBITDA from two miners is difficult unless the underlying definitions are aligned.

Management describes producing Bitcoin at a structural discount to market prices. Miners can achieve attractive unit costs when power contracts, fleet efficiency and site economics are favorable, but network difficulty and Bitcoin's price constantly change the equation. Electricity costs can be seasonal; new mining capacity can increase competition; older machines can become less efficient. A production model that looks profitable today may be less attractive after a difficult market shift. It is useful to track the cost of mining one coin, but readers should ask exactly which direct and indirect costs are included.

Public shareholders own claims on a company, not specific coins in its reserve. That provides exposure to mining operations and management decisions as well as the Bitcoin held. The company can create value through efficient production and disciplined capital allocation, but it can also face stock-market volatility unrelated to the daily Bitcoin move. The most meaningful comparison with direct BTC ownership is not merely whether its reserves rise, but whether shareholders benefit after dilution, corporate costs and valuation premiums are considered.

The next quarter should be assessed using mined BTC, treasury changes, production cost, network difficulty, share count and cash liquidity. The company's earnings release is a useful snapshot, but it does not make future mining profitability predictable. In a sector where several operators are selling Bitcoin to fund AI data centers, American Bitcoin's stated emphasis on accumulating coins provides a distinct business model for investors to evaluate.

Primary source: American Bitcoin Corp third-quarter announcement. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s coverage of capital moving through regulated Bitcoin investments.

#5221. Greece Passes Crypto Tax Law With 10% Gains Levy, 15% on Staking and DeFi

Greece Passes Crypto Tax Law With 10% Gains Levy, 15% on Staking and DeFi

Greece's parliament passed a bill Thursday imposing a 10% capital-gains tax on cryptocurrency profits, marking a shift from a proposal discussed in October to legislation approved by lawmakers.

The measure also puts a 15% tax on income from staking, crypto lending and decentralized finance, or DeFi.

The legislation’s crypto provisions sit inside a wider Finance Ministry package dealing with debt servicing, financial oversight, and government accounting, with the new crypto income-tax rules scheduled for January 1, 2027.

Individual investors with annual net crypto gains of €500 ($557) or less will be exempt. Cross that threshold, though, and the 10% tax applies to the entire gain, rather than just the amount over €500 ($557).

For example, an investor with €600 ($669) in net gains would face a €60 ($67) tax under the new rate, not €10 ($11). The rules generally calculate gains by subtracting the acquisition cost from the value received when the crypto is disposed of.

Selling Bitcoin for euros is one taxable event, but spending crypto on goods or services can also count as a disposal. Investors will need transaction records to establish what they paid for their assets and what those assets were worth when sold or spent.

Yield from staking, lending, and providing liquidity to DeFi protocols is treated differently. Greece will classify those returns as interest income and tax them at 15%, according to the Finance Ministry.

Qualifying crypto losses exceeding €500 ($557) can be carried forward for five years and offset against future crypto gains. The bill also gives investors a 12-month window to voluntarily declare previously unreported crypto income without late-filing penalties or interest, provided they meet its conditions and pay within 60 days of filing.

The passage follows an October public consultation on the proposal.The draft proposed a 10% rate that was already lower than the 15% levy Greek officials had discussed in June.

Athens says exchanging one cryptocurrency for another should not itself trigger a capital-gains tax. But the wording lawmakers approved may not make that exclusion as clear as the government intended.

A review by parliament's Scientific Service flagged the bill's broad language covering disposals made "for consideration." That phrase could include token-to-token trades, the review said, even though the accompanying explanation says such swaps should not create taxable gains.

The reviewers recommended an explicit exemption and suggested clearer rules for exchanges involving stablecoins and electronic-money tokens. Those details matter for traders who rotate between assets without cashing out to fiat currency.

Greece is also preparing the administrative rules needed to put the tax into practice. A senior Finance Ministry official told Reuters that guidance on transaction transparency and tax audits would be issued in the coming weeks.

The government has not estimated how much the tax will raise. Officials say the market is difficult to measure because many Greek crypto investors use trading platforms based overseas.

#5220. Shiba Inu price analysis — Analyzing if SHIB buyers can defend $0.00000426

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Like much of the cryptocurrency market, Shiba Inu, SHIB, has been facing selling pressure lately, with the token down 1.76% to $0.00000439 over the past 24 hours. The same was the case with Bitcoin, with the total crypto market capitalization down by 1.98%.

The wider market’s sell-off is a cumulative reaction to a multitude of factors that have unfolded worldwide over the past week, primarily caused by political uncertainty post-U.S. midterms, Fed rate hike fears, and geopolitical tensions. Additionally, as SHIB is a high-beta memecoin, it has followed in the footsteps of Bitcoin’s decline. Subdued ecosystem activity has offered little support so far.

Nevertheless, buyers could be attempting to defend the $0.00000426-support level.

SHIB’s daily chart showcased its bearish trend. However, early signs of a recovery seemed to be forming too. At the time of writing, the altcoin was trading at $0.00000440, within the lower end of the 0%–23.6% Fibonacci retracement zone. Despite sustained selling pressure, buyers have been attempting to defend the $0.00000426 support level.

Momentum indicators suggested that bearish pressure, while in place, had started to ease too. The MACD was still below both its Signal and Zero lines, pointing to a bearish trend. However, the narrowing gap between the two lines suggested that the selling momentum could be losing strength.

Additionally, the recent uptick in OBV indicated that buying interest had started to grow again, especially around these press time levels. However, this needs to be sustained and accompanied by stronger volume before a more convincing recovery can take shape.

The 4-hour chart lent further insight into the altcoin’s press time price action, with SHIB attempting to break above the 23.6% Fibonacci retracement level. A successful move above this resistance would make a short-term recovery more likely. Especially as it suggested that buyers were becoming more willing to step in. However, the level still needs to be reclaimed for the sentiment to shift.

The MACD showed an early improvement, with its line turning upwards towards the Signal line, although both remained below zero. This suggested that despite selling pressure starting to decline, bearish momentum would still be dominant.

Furthermore, the OBV also ticked higher on the charts, hinting at a fresh wave of buying activity.

Together, these signals suggested that buyers have been making an attempt to build momentum. However, recovery will remain tentative without stronger volume and a sustained move above resistance.

#5219. Charles Schwab Expands Crypto Trading to SOL, AVAX and LINK, XRP Not Included

American brokerage giant Charles Schwab has announced that it is expanding its crypto trading platform, Schwab Crypto, with the addition of Solana (SOL), Avalanche (AVAX) and Chainlink (LINK) for trading.

The three new coins expand the existing list of two cryptocurrencies, Bitcoin (BTC) and Ethereum (ETH), that can be traded directly. This now means that users can trade five cryptocurrencies directly on the platform.

Notably, however, XRP, one of the largest cryptocurrencies by market cap, is still missing from the list of available coins.

In its official press release, Schwab's head of digital assets, Joe Vietri, underlined the brokerage's commitment to further expanding its crypto trading options.

Also, the company confirmed that it will be adding more cryptocurrencies and digital assets going forward. But it didn't specify whether this includes XRP.

Although XRP is not offered directly on the Schwab Crypto trading platform, it is still available to Schwab users in one way or another.

Charles Schwab offers standard and micro futures contracts on XRP through its thinkorswim platform.

In July 2025, the company pointed out the availability of XRP futures with the ticker /XRP and also smaller micro contracts with the ticker /MXP.

It further expanded its derivatives trading platform with the addition of almost 24/7 trading for certain crypto futures, including Bitcoin, Ethereum, Solana and XRP, in June 2026.

Charles Schwab's aggressive approach to the crypto industry has been noticeable throughout 2026.

First, the company officially announced Schwab Crypto on April 16. In this announcement, it outlined its plans to introduce direct trading of Bitcoin and Ethereum for retail users.

The next major development came on Oct. 15, when Rick Wurster, the CEO of Charles Schwab, confirmed the introduction of crypto transfers.

The platform now allows eligible customers to transfer eligible crypto assets to and from external wallets through the Schwab Crypto platform. However, this is not yet available to all customers. Access is being rolled out gradually.

#5218. Re Protocol Tops $400M in Deposits as Onchain Reinsurance Yield Attracts Capital

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Re Protocol says deposits across its reUSD and reUSDe products have exceeded $400 million, putting a relatively unfamiliar type of real-world asset exposure into the spotlight: insurance and reinsurance. Its November 4 announcement says the latest $100 million arrived in 40 days, about 80% faster than the previous comparable step.

Re Protocol says deposits across its reUSD and reUSDe products have exceeded $400 million, putting a relatively unfamiliar type of real-world asset exposure into the spotlight: insurance and reinsurance. Its November 4 announcement says the latest $100 million arrived in 40 days, about 80% faster than the previous comparable step. The growth is notable, but investors need to understand what supports the yield before treating the products as another ordinary stablecoin account.

The protocol says combined deposits into its two offerings have passed $400 million and that it has accrued more than $13 million in yield for users. These are figures supplied by the project, not an independent measure of long-run investment performance. Deposits are also different from revenue, profit or insurance premiums earned. A rapid increase in capital can reflect demand for a new product, incentives or movements between competing yield strategies. That makes the pace of growth relevant without proving that the underlying returns can be maintained as the pool becomes larger or market conditions deteriorate.

Re Protocol's approach links digital asset capital to insurance-related risk. Reinsurance companies are paid for absorbing portions of risks originally taken by insurers, so returns ultimately depend on underwriting, the frequency and severity of claims, contract structures and the reserves kept to meet obligations. That risk does not behave exactly like lending stablecoins against volatile crypto collateral. A quiet claims period can look attractive; a major insured event or unexpected loss development may expose vulnerabilities that were not visible in recent annualized performance figures. For depositors, understanding who bears losses is more important than a headline rate.

The company cites seven-day annualized yields of 6.82% for reUSD and 11.82% for reUSDe as of October 30. A seven-day observation is a short sample, not a fixed annual coupon or a promised payout. Rates can reflect product design, incentives, risk profile and the timing of income recognition. Comparing one yield to a bank savings rate or a government bond without accounting for custody, redemption, liquidity and underwriting exposure would create a misleading impression of the trade-off. Investors should examine disclosures on withdrawal mechanics, reserve treatment, any protective structures and how losses are allocated.

Putting insurance-related strategies on a blockchain can simplify accounting, transfers and access for eligible participants, but it also introduces smart contract and operational dependencies. A digital token representing an interest in a strategy is not automatically the same as a bank deposit, a fully collateralized dollar or a publicly traded security. The legal rights attached to the token matter if a strategy enters stress or if liquidity dries up. Independent contract assessments, custody arrangements and transparent reporting may improve confidence, yet none can guarantee against unexpected claims or structural mismatches.

The next meaningful disclosures would include sustained capital retention, breakdowns of yield sources, realized claims experience, audit scope, redemption performance and changes in the size of the risk pool. Those numbers would help determine whether Re Protocol is building a durable bridge between regulated insurance and decentralized markets or merely benefiting from a temporary appetite for yield. The onchain insurance thesis is worth watching, but the key question is what depositors are actually being paid to bear.

Primary source: Re Protocol company announcement. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s reporting on institutional investors taking exposure to DeFi markets.

#5217. This Cardano Sweep Area Puts ADA Reversal In Motion

Home » Cardano (ADA) » This Cardano Sweep Area Puts ADA Reversal In Motion

The OG altcoin’s under-performance price-wise can reverse with ADA nailing the mid-line Bollinger Band.

Cardano’s (ADA) price took in a 28% deficit over the past 30 days, now closely approaching the lowest-tier Bollinger Band (BOLL) at $0.1735. On the 4-hour charts, Cardano’s (ADA) price has been trading well below all four moving averages in the ribbon, carrying no bullish momentum until ADA restores $0.1811.

That’s the midline Bolinger Band (BOLL), which also coincides with the lowest moving-average calculated trendline in the ribbon. A sweep into the $0.166 – $0.171 area followed by strong buying could signal a gradual Cardano price reversal setup, a common pattern in volume profile and Fibonacci-based trading strategies.

Strong confluence building on $ADA.Range POC + 0.786 Fib + local liquidity all stacked around $0.166–$0.171.If price sweeps that zone and gets bought back, the reversal setup gets very interesting. pic.twitter.com/6xXpvhaMT6

Cardano’s (ADA) bulls won’t be able to regain territory unless the altcoin sustains above $0.214 on the 4-hour & daily time-frames, a level unprecedented since October 25, 2026, according to the real-time data from TradingView. The 4% price dip comes along with the broader market tarnishing over $150 billion in value overnight.

Presently,  the liquidity zone around $0.166 – $0.171 represents the strongest demand territory. It combines the range POC (Point of Control), the 0.786 Fibonacci retracement ADA price level & the local liquidity on regular Spot markets.

Meanwhile, Futures markets players have been betting on a rebound, but incurred a heavy liquidation instead. That made the bulls more careful.

Cardano’s (ADA) $6,686,156,790 market capitalization put the altcoin at #18, chasing behind major-cap competitors Chainlink (LINK) & Dogecoin (DOGE) by $2 and more than $4 billion, respectively. The OG coin’s Fully-Diluted Valuation (FDV) stood at $8.02 billion, per SoSoValue.

#5216. Ripple's Schwartz Debunks XRP Price Myth

David Schwartz, the former chief technology officer at Ripple and one of the creators of the XRP Ledger (XRPL), has dispelled one of the most common myths regarding the key factors that could impact the price of XRP.

The former Ripple CTO took to the X social media platform to challenge the idea that the use of XRP as the native gas token of the XRP Ledger could be a major factor in determining the price of the cryptocurrency.

"I don't think anybody who knows anything has said that the use of XRP as gas will be a major driver of the price of XRP," Schwartz wrote.

The comment was made as the Ripple veteran took part in the ever-growing debate centered around the poor price performance of XRP and the place of the cryptocurrency in Ripple's ever-expanding ecosystem.

The former Ripple CTO directed the audience's attention to a post from November 2025, in which he explained his thoughts on how mass adoption of the XRP Ledger could lead to greater value for the ledger's native cryptocurrency.

"The increased use and adoption of XRPL will add value to XRP by its privileged place on the ledger," Schwartz said.

He emphasized that XRP has several unique properties, including the lack of counterparty risk, the fact that it's jurisdictionless, and its privileged place in the network's autobridging features.

In addition, Schwartz argued that it would be hard to imagine the XRP Ledger succeeding on a mass scale without XRP capturing a significant portion of the value created by such success.

"I really do think it's hard to think we could end up in a world where XRPL is successful and XRP doesn't get a good fraction of that value," he said.

Nevertheless, Schwartz also said earlier that the XRP Ledger was not created with the sole intent of pushing the price of XRP higher. Instead, the original goal was to create a fast and cheap payment system with a native digital asset and a decentralized exchange feature.

Schwartz's recent comments come as frustration among XRP holders over the poor performance of the cryptocurrency is on the rise.

As of Monday, the Ripple-related token was trading at around the $1.09 level, down more than 12% in the last seven days.

Recently, Schwartz admitted that his influence on the market price of XRP is very small.

"Do I have anything to do with the price of XRP? Honestly? I guess probably not anything. But very little," he wrote during another interaction with critics.

#5215. Coinbase wins final OCC approval – Why COIN stock remains under pressure

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Coinbase has finally received the receipt for the final approval for a national trust bank charter. The Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), the U.S. regulator responsible for supervising national banks, granted Coinbase final approval last week, following conditional approval in April 2026.

That said, the initial approval meant Coinbase had met certain requirements to move forward, but it still needed to address outstanding regulatory conditions before receiving full authorization. But over the following months, Coinbase took all the steps to satisfy the OCC’s requirements for operating as a national trust bank.

Given the approval, the charter will now allow Coinbase to provide crypto custody services and develop payment-related operations under a federal regulatory framework.

That said, the authorization would also support Coinbase’s stablecoin operations, including issuing tokens and managing stablecoins for other companies. However, the approval does not turn Coinbase into a conventional commercial bank.

It’s important to note that Coinbase National Trust cannot accept deposits from ordinary customers or provide traditional commercial loans.

As of this, Coinbase has now joined Ripple, Revolut, Coinbase, Paxos, BitGo, and Circle, who have already gotten the green light for National Trust Bank. Yet despite this, the stock price of Coinbase ($COIN) was trading at $134.76 after a drop of 1.51% in the past trading day.

This might be because the OCC’s decision to grant national trust bank charters to crypto companies has faced opposition from traditional banking groups. The Independent Community Bankers of America recently sued the regulator, questioning its legal authority to issue certain charters to digital asset firms.

Meanwhile, the SEC too has proposed changes to existing broker-dealer regulations covering capital requirements, customer asset protection, and recordkeeping for firms handling digital assets.

This followed Coinbase securing a legal victory in New Jersey after regulators dropped their lawsuit over its staking services and lifted the cease-and-desist order.

Additionally, Coinbase also recorded $719 million in net income in Q3 2026, compared with $433 million a year earlier. Adjusted EBITDA also reached $333 million, up 60% quarter-over-quarter, marking its 15th consecutive profitable quarter on this metric.

#5214. Bybit Says $17.3B Proof of Reserves Covers 50 Assets, but the Audit Has Limits

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Bybit says the latest snapshot of its proof-of-reserves system covered $17.3 billion in customer assets across 50 cryptocurrencies. The exchange's November 3 announcement describes its 41st report, with figures independently checked by security firm Hacken against an October 27 snapshot.

Bybit says the latest snapshot of its proof-of-reserves system covered $17.3 billion in customer assets across 50 cryptocurrencies. The exchange's November 3 announcement describes its 41st report, with figures independently checked by security firm Hacken against an October 27 snapshot. The exercise offers a view of assets and customer balances at a specific moment, but it should not be mistaken for an all-purpose guarantee that an exchange could meet every liability under every scenario.

Bybit says its reserve ratios exceeded 100% across all 50 covered assets, including 112% for USDT, 405% for USDC, 102% for Bitcoin and 101% for Ether. Those percentages compare identified reserves with customer balances under the stated methodology. An unusually high ratio for one token does not mean the business has the same excess in every asset or that reserves could be freely moved between customer pools. Readers should also distinguish a token-by-token reserve ratio from consolidated corporate solvency, which incorporates obligations that may not appear in a proof-of-reserves snapshot.

The release identifies the audit point as October 27 at 06:00 UTC. Reserve balances, withdrawals, borrowing, market prices and off-exchange exposures may have changed after that time. The value of recurring disclosures lies partly in their comparability: users can look for persistent gaps or unexpected movements rather than treating a single publication as permanent reassurance. Bybit says the checks covered the 50 listed crypto assets, but the exact perimeter, included customer categories and handling of institutional arrangements are questions worth reviewing in the detailed report.

The exchange says Hacken carried out the verification. Third-party involvement is useful, particularly when the reviewer explains its methods and limitations, but the word 'audit' can be interpreted too broadly. A Merkle-tree exercise may allow customers to verify that a balance was included while proving possession of identified onchain assets. It does not, by itself, necessarily establish that there are no undisclosed debts, encumbrances, affiliated-party claims or operational weaknesses. The scope of work therefore deserves as much attention as the name of the verifier.

Asset backing is a fundamental question for custodial exchanges because users depend on the firm to return assets they cannot directly control. Yet security controls, market surveillance, legal ownership of customer property, segregation practices and withdrawal resilience also matter. A fully funded snapshot is only one component of a broader risk picture. The report can be especially informative when combined with clear explanations of liabilities, independent financial statements where available and timely incident reporting. It should not substitute for personal decisions about custodial concentration.

Bybit users can inspect the methodology and verify balances if the relevant tool supports that check. External analysts should compare successive reports and look for consistency between claimed assets and observable onchain holdings. Markets benefit from more systematic disclosures, but transparent limitations are an important part of credible transparency. The milestone is the reporting process, not a declaration that counterparty risk has disappeared.

Primary source: Bybit proof-of-reserves announcement. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s analysis of earnings and stablecoin activity at another major exchange.

#5213. Coinbase Opens Tokenized Gold-Powered Loans

Home » Blitz news » Coinbase Opens Tokenized Gold-Powered Loans

The gold-backed loans run on a new USDC/PAXG market on Morpho Blue, deployed on Base.

Eligible Coinbase users in the United States can now borrow USDC using Paxos’s PAX Gold as collateral. The feature, which went live in early November, marks the first time Coinbase Borrow has accepted a non-crypto asset.

Until now, Coinbase Borrow had only accepted crypto-native collateral such as Bitcoin, Ether, XRP, and Solana. PAXG, each token representing one fine troy ounce of physical gold held in LBMA-accredited vaults, becomes the first real-world asset on the platform.

Millions of Coinbase users can now borrow USDC against Paxos's tokenized gold, powered by Morpho.Gold is the first non-crypto collateral in Coinbase Borrow, which has ~$3.4B of collateral and $1.7B+ in loans on Morpho.@Morpho pic.twitter.com/CpwST0wJnJ

Users can pledge the token inside the Coinbase app and receive USDC without selling their gold position.

Coinbase supplies the user interface and customer base; Morpho supplies the on-chain lending infrastructure and liquidity.

The same integration already supports roughly $3.4 billion in collateral and more than $1.7 billion in active loans across crypto assets. Adding gold required no new lending stack—only a new market on the existing system.

Borrowers face variable interest rates set by the Morpho market rather than by Coinbase. There is no fixed repayment schedule or minimum payment.

*COINBASE & PNC EXPAND STABLECOIN PAYMENT PARTNERSHIP*COINBASE: ELIGIBLE PNC CLIENTS CAN RECEIVE PAYMENTS IN USDC

Positions are governed by loan-to-value thresholds: if the value of the pledged PAXG falls too far relative to the outstanding USDC, the collateral can be liquidated.

Access is limited to eligible U.S. customers outside New York, with asset-specific borrowing limits and terms displayed in the app.

Only a few billion dollars of the roughly $30 trillion gold market has been tokenized so far. Coinbase’s move gives holders of one of the larger regulated gold tokens a way to raise dollar liquidity against it without leaving the exchange.

#5212. XRP, Near Protocol (NEAR), Hyperliquid (HYPE) and Stellar (XLM) Price Analysis for November 10: Market Pressure Keeps Growing

With price currently trading at $1.096, XRP has already recorded another substantial drop. The market positioning of the asset remains weak, as XRP is trading below its major moving averages. Higher lows and fundamental improvements are two main priorities for the asset right now.

XRP's last bullish hope was a short recovery rally towards the $1.34 price threshold. It failed to hold on to that momentum and tumbled towards the area where buyers previously stopped the summer decline. From approximately $1.34, XRP lost roughly 18% of its value and lost everything it built up throughout the recovery.

But where would XRP finally settle down? Realistically, the closest support is the $1.075 level, matching the latest session's low, followed by the $1.03–$1.05 range and the $0.99–$1.00 area, both psychological and technical. A breakdown below, unfortunately, would put the summer bottom under pressure and open the way to further weakness.

But even if XRP is oversold, with RSI below 30, bulls might still stay sidelined. The XRP Ledger activated the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment on November 4. It removed an exploit that, under certain conditions, let accounts authorized to burn an issuer's tokens also mint them. The native supply of XRP has not been affected, luckily, but the amendment is not a factor that can help XRP reach key market levels.

In a case, where bulls somehow wake up, a recovery towards $1.15 and $1.20 is the main priority, while a proper trend reversal would require a move toward the moving averages around $1.30–$1.35.

Whether buyers can defend the $1 level or not is the main test for the asset right now. Without substantial volume, a short-lived price bounce here won't change the situation as a whole, unfortunately.

NEAR entered November under pressure after losing much of its September advance. After briefly reaching approximately $6 in October, the token fell toward $3.66 on November 9, according to the daily NEAR/USDT chart.

That represented a decline of roughly 39% from the local peak and put NEAR back at an important moving-average support zone.

Trading activity has also weakened alongside the price. Volume remains substantially below the levels seen during September’s breakout, suggesting that buyers have not yet returned with comparable strength.

However, lower volume does not necessarily confirm that selling pressure has disappeared. NEAR continues to form lower highs, while recovery attempts remain below its shorter-term moving averages.

Institutional exposure remains part of the investment case. Bitwise’s spot NEAR ETF, launched on September 29, held approximately 15.69 million NEAR worth $58.21 million as of Nov. 5. The fund reported that 99% of its holdings were staked.

Hyperliquid keeps going down and has recently violated an important moving average. It's now trading at $75, going further into downtrend, despite the strong fundamental background around Hyperliquid: Hyperliquid Strategies disclosure and a potential approval by the U.S. futures commission.

The broader market access, however, isn't something that the majority of retail investors consider a bullish catalyst, unfortunately. The asset has not yet found a strong bullish case. It's unlikely to be institutional too.

Technically, the bounce of HYPE/USDT could happen on 200 EMA, especially with a volume uptick and the recovery of the market in general. However, the selling pressure still overweights the buying support, according to exchange flows.

The netflow stays negative, and Hyperliquid is only yet to catch up with the bullish flow of the market. RSI confirms the thesis, moving around 40 and suggesting a bearish momentum, or a sideways movement at least. Volume should also surge substantially.

Key price levels for HYPE are: $72–$73 as the closest support level, $68 as the longer-term moving average, and $78–$80 as the first resistance level. Several moving averages sit at approximately $81–$85, so a breakthrough there should become a foundation for a price recovery.

Stellar already lost an important psychological level of $0.15, and there's a real scenario where XLM breaks down even more.

37% is the current recorded loss from its September peak of $0.235. The correction is rapidly erasing the recovery it has been building and finally invalidating it in October. The price is back in the accumulation range that was present back in May.

XLM moves below all major moving averages, with most of them aiming downwards and hinting at the aggravation of the current trend on the market.

With RSI and volume moving sideways or showing a descent, bear trend keeps the upper hand with sustained pressure on the price going further. The only positive signal could be the fact that RSI temporarily violated the reading of 30, which could be the sign of XLM approaching an oversold zone.

From a fundamental perspective, the Bell Protocol upgrade number 30 is the only thing that could set XLM apart from the rest of the market, with the vote scheduled for November 23.

The update will introduce: muxed contract addresses (ability to serve multiple users from a single smart contract), post-quantum signature verification and more.

In order to make the network more responsive, resilient and stronger in the future, millisecond ledger timing is also coming. It won't make the network faster on its own after the upgrade, but it opens up possibilities for developers to improve the network in the future.

#5211. Hyperliquid loses KEY support – Can a bear trap send HYPE to $100?

Is Hyperliquid’s [HYPE] current breakdown a potential bear trap before a $100 breakout?

If HYPE cannot hold this, then $64 is the next key level to watch, followed by $53. However, with HYPE already trading below the $75 level following a 2% intraday dip, the technical picture is becoming more bearish.

Naturally, this raises the question: Is a drop toward $53 becoming more likely?

Binance has now reopened the withdrawal of HYPE tokens after the $6.3 billion hack. This means that users will be able to move HYPE off the exchange. If withdrawals pick up, exchange balances could decline, potentially tightening the available supply and creating conditions for a supply squeeze.

So, if this trend holds, could bulls flip the bearish Hyperliquid setup into a classic bear trap and catch late shorts off guard? With overall market sentiment in extreme fear, an unexpected reversal could trigger a sharp short squeeze, potentially setting HYPE up for a repeat of its late-July rally.

This is a buyback process where HYPE is purchased using revenue from the protocol. The HYPE is then destroyed and removed completely from circulation.

More importantly, the pace of buybacks could increase if protocol revenue continues to rise.

As shown on the chart, Hyperliquid’s revenue has risen by 20% in under 24 hours, adding nearly $290k. Higher revenue could translate into larger buybacks, which can increase the amount of HYPE being removed from supply. This can provide further support for a recovery if the altcoin begins to see a rise in demand.

This is where HYPE’s historical price action and Binance’s withdrawal reopening become important. The continued buybacks and possible shortages mean a drop down to $53 is too much of a stretch. Instead, the breakdown could result in a bear trap, which catches late shorts and creates a July-style rally. Thus, a move toward $100 could still be on the cards for November.

#5210. Solana Changelog Puts Faster Web3.js Builds and Validator Work in Focus

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Solana's November 5 developer changelog highlights a collection of tooling and validator improvements rather than one headline-grabbing network upgrade. Among them are faster builds for JavaScript applications, changes affecting validator operations and further work around execution tools.

Solana's November 5 developer changelog highlights a collection of tooling and validator improvements rather than one headline-grabbing network upgrade. Among them are faster builds for JavaScript applications, changes affecting validator operations and further work around execution tools. For developers, these smaller improvements can influence how quickly applications ship and how reliably infrastructure behaves. For investors, the distinction is that a published development update is not necessarily a newly activated change on mainnet.

One item in the changelog concerns the JavaScript SDK build process using Rolldown. The developers describe a reduction in an internal build benchmark from around two seconds to almost zero. That is a promising productivity claim, but it applies to the specified workflow and benchmark rather than every Solana app suddenly loading faster for users. Faster library builds can improve testing and release cycles, particularly for teams maintaining many packages. Its end-user effect depends on whether downstream developers adopt the updated tools and whether the gains carry over to their own projects.

The update also points to work on the Agave validator client, typed interfaces and elements of Firedancer leader handover. These terms describe software and operational changes within the infrastructure that keeps the chain running. Their practical significance lies in reliability, interoperability and the conditions under which different validator implementations coordinate. However, a changelog reference should not be treated as evidence that every validator has deployed the work or that a planned network feature has passed all activation checks. Operators need specific release notes, configuration guidance and rollout schedules.

The changelog includes improvements involving Anchor, RPC-related tools and local development environments such as Surfpool. Anchor is widely used for building Solana programs, while RPC services provide the interface through which wallets, apps and analytics products query the chain. Small optimizations in account allocation or local testing can save developer time and reduce avoidable mistakes. They do not remove the need for security review, careful transaction handling or capacity planning. Because Solana applications rely on several layers, a weakness at the RPC or front-end level can still affect users even when core consensus works normally.

Solana has long emphasized throughput and latency, but engineering benchmarks depend on hardware, software versions and the specific task measured. An SDK build benchmark is not a transaction-per-second statistic, while validator handover work is not a promise of zero outages. Keeping those categories separate helps readers understand what the team has actually improved. Adoption may eventually show up in developer retention, easier deployment and more dependable applications, but those effects take time and should be measured rather than assumed from a release note.

Developers can follow the linked repositories, testing results and future rollout notices for each change. The clearest evidence of impact would be adoption in major tools, reproducible performance measurements and fewer operational headaches for app builders and node operators. The November update reflects continuous ecosystem maintenance—often less dramatic than a large protocol announcement, but essential to whether Solana can support more demanding financial applications over time.

Primary source: Solana developer changelog. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s recent review of Solana’s October development milestones.

#5209. Treasury Cuts Crypto Exchange MEXC Off From US Financial System

Treasury Cuts Crypto Exchange MEXC Off From US Financial System

The U.S. Treasury Department has moved to sever crypto exchange MEXC Global from the American financial system, accusing it of facilitating at least $16 billion in illicit transactions tied to Iran, North Korea and Russia.

In an order issued Friday through its Financial Crimes Enforcement Network, Treasury said MEXC, one of the world’s 20 largest crypto exchanges, served as a major conduit for sanctions evasion and cybercrime.

The action, part of a broader effort dubbed Operation Economic Outcast, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing any funds transfers to or from the exchange. It takes effect 30 days after publication in the Federal Register.

“Financial institutions cannot have it both ways: Either you support the Iranian regime, or you have access to the U.S. financial system,” Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said in a statement, vowing to keep severing the regime’s “economic lifelines” and its attempts to use digital assets to dodge sanctions.

Founded in 2018, MEXC processes more than $1 billion in daily transactions, holds around $5 billion in reserves, and offers more than 3,000 tokens, according to FinCEN. Between May 2021 and May 2026, FinCEN said, the platform moved at least $16 billion connected to illicit activity, including nearly $1.5 billion tied to Russian sanctions evasion, with $232 million in direct transactions with sanctioned entities. The agency named MEXC a “primary money laundering concern” in connection with Russian illicit finance.

Despite publicly claiming anti-money-laundering controls and barring users in places like Iran and North Korea, MEXC’s compliance was riddled with “severe deficiencies,” FinCEN said.

The exchange was still transacting with sanctioned Russian exchange Garantex and its successor Grinex as recently as June 2026, more than four years after Garantex was first designated. It also processed roughly $200 million linked to the A7A5 stablecoin and about $22 million benefiting Iran’s central bank and its Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps.

Those connections place MEXC squarely within a sprawling sanctions-evasion ecosystem that Treasury has been dismantling piece by piece.

The department last month designated Russia’s A7 Network a transnational criminal organization and proposed cutting its front companies off from U.S. finance, flagging more than $179 billion in transactions through its ruble-backed A7A5 token, much of it routed through Garantex and Grinex.

The same campaign has reached into Iran’s crypto sector, where Treasury gained sweeping authority to sanction anyone operating in it and blacklisted the Iranian exchange Nobitex over its role in funneling crypto to the IRGC.

The order uses a provision of the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act rather than a standard sanctions designation, a tool FinCEN has increasingly reached for to cut off crypto infrastructure that operates outside the correspondent banking system.

#5208. Publicly Traded Bitcoin Miner Expands BTC Holdings Despite Market Crash

Publicly traded Bitcoin mining giant BitFuFu, based in Singapore, has massively increased its Bitcoin holdings in spite of the current crypto market crash.

Bitcoin Treasuries data shows that the Nasdaq-listed mining firm, trading under the ticker FUFU, added 66 BTC to its Bitcoin holdings. This brings the total Bitcoin held by the company to a massive 1,454 BTC.

As per Bitcoin Treasuries data, BitFuFu now ranks 38th in the Bitcoin 100 following the recent increase in its Bitcoin holdings.

BitFuFu’s increase in Bitcoin holdings comes at a time when the overall cryptocurrency market has been hit by a massive sell-off in the past few days.

However, it is worth noting that the rise in Bitcoin holdings by BitFuFu is as of Oct. 31, before the recent increase in selling pressure in the crypto market.

The Singaporean mining firm reported that it had 1,388 BTC in its holdings by the end of September in its operational update dated Nov. 6. This means its Bitcoin holdings in October showed a 4.8% month-over-month increase.

Three consecutive months of increases in Bitcoin holdings by the Singaporean Bitcoin mining giant are evidence that the company is not backing down from increasing its holdings in the top cryptocurrency.

BitFuFu’s latest increase in its Bitcoin holdings comes amid a massive drop in the company’s mining output.

In its latest operational report, BitFuFu reported that it mined 137 BTC in October compared to 167 BTC mined in September. This shows that the company’s mining output decreased by around 18%.

Out of the total 137 BTC mined in October, 67 BTC were mined through self-mining, while 70 BTC were mined through cloud mining.

The reduction in mining output by BitFuFu was mainly due to a huge increase in Bitcoin mining difficulty, which increased by around 11.8% in October.

However, BitFuFu was able to increase its Bitcoin holdings while keeping its mining power relatively stable.

In October, the total hashrate managed by BitFuFu was 19.5 exahashes per second (EH/s). This is compared to 19.4 EH/s recorded in September.

BitFuFu Chairman and CEO Leo Lu reiterates commitment to increasing Bitcoin holdings.

#5206. Circle Makes the Case for AI Agent Payments, With Spending Controls at the Center

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Circle is arguing that stablecoins could become a useful payment layer for AI agents that buy software, access services or settle transactions on behalf of people and businesses. A November 4 explainer from the USDC issuer sets out what it calls agentic commerce, a concept that would allow authorized software to initiate purchases within predefined rules.

Circle is arguing that stablecoins could become a useful payment layer for AI agents that buy software, access services or settle transactions on behalf of people and businesses. A November 4 explainer from the USDC issuer sets out what it calls agentic commerce, a concept that would allow authorized software to initiate purchases within predefined rules. It is a vision for a developing market, not evidence that an autonomous AI payments network has already reached mass adoption.

The basic idea is to let a software agent complete a purchase after receiving instructions and permission from a human or business. An agent might compare pricing, obtain a computing service, pay a provider and record the transaction without asking a person to click through each step. This differs from a chatbot that only recommends products or from an automatic recurring subscription. Stablecoins are appealing in this setting because value can move over internet-native rails and settle across borders, but those features are useful only when an agent can prove what it is authorized to do.

The central design challenge is delegated authority. A business needs to specify which agent may spend, how much, on which categories of supplier and for what period. It also needs authentication, records of the instruction that triggered a payment and a way to revoke authority. Without those safeguards, automated payments can turn minor software errors or malicious prompts into real financial losses. Circle's discussion points to the infrastructure opportunity, but a stablecoin transfer alone cannot determine whether the underlying purchase was legitimate or met the user's expectations.

USDC and other dollar-linked tokens could allow small cross-border payments and programmable transfers that are difficult to reproduce with traditional card infrastructure. However, blockchain transfers can be difficult to reverse. Conventional card payments often involve dispute rights, fraud monitoring and customer-support processes that do not automatically accompany an onchain transfer. A workable agent payment service therefore needs explicit handling for refunds, incorrect goods, failed delivery and disputes. It must also address sanctions checks, identity requirements and jurisdictional limits where relevant. Technology that moves money quickly cannot replace those business obligations.

Developers are experimenting with AI systems that interact with tools, APIs and online marketplaces. As those systems gain the ability to perform tasks rather than simply produce text, a reliable method of paying for digital services becomes more relevant. Stablecoin issuers and payment processors see potential demand, but practical uptake depends on merchants accepting the method and users trusting the controls. The most credible demonstrations will involve narrow, verifiable use cases rather than broad claims that intelligent agents can safely manage unlimited funds.

Payment providers will need to show who bears liability when an agent makes a mistaken purchase, how permissions are audited and how customers recover from fraud. They will also need to show cost advantages after fees, compliance and integration work are included. Circle's essay is best read as an articulation of a developing architecture, not a new product launch. The long-term test is whether automated payments become easier for ordinary businesses without making financial control harder.

Primary source: Circle analysis of agentic commerce. For additional context, see NewsBTC’s report on a consumer-facing USDC payment integration.

#5205. Coachella 2027 Lineup Leaks: Polymarket Odds Reveal the Top 3 Favorites

Home » Crypto Market News » Coachella 2027 Lineup Leaks: Polymarket Odds Reveal the Top 3 Favorites

Industry leaks point to major pop and rock icons—here is who prediction markets expect to officially take the stage.

Rumors about the Coachella 2027 lineup picked up steam after reports and social media posts pointed to SZA, Dua Lipa, and Tame Impala as the festival’s possible headliners.

While the music festival’s promoter Goldenvoice has yet to confirm any performers, crypto-based prediction markets reacted instantly to the industry buzz.

On Polymarket, the leading Web3 prediction platform, capital flowed rapidly into event contracts as crypto traders rushed to price in the latest reporting.

Industry buzz began picking up momentum after reports from media outlets, including TMZ, indicated that talks were underway with Dua Lipa and Tame Impala.

The speculation spiked further when R&B star SZA seemingly corroborated the rumors online, pointing toward a headlining trio representing three distinct corners of modern pop, R&B, and psychedelic rock.

Reports also suggest that festival organizers had approached other massive acts—including BTS, Oasis, Radiohead, and Rage Against the Machine—before cementing focus on the current trio.

Crypto-based prediction markets have become a real-time barometer of public sentiment when entertainment leaks surface. The rumors have also played out on Polymarket, where odds shift instantly as new information emerges.

Polymarket’s latest odds show strong confidence in the leaked trio.

As of November 10, SZA has a 74% implied probability of appearing on the lineup. Dua Lipa follows at 65%, with $9,917 in trading volume—the highest individual total among all listed artists—marking a potential milestone for her first solo Coachella headlining performance. Tame Impala sits close behind at 61%.

Beyond the top three, other artists are also drawing bets. BLACKPINK has a 45% implied probability, followed by Drake at 28%.

Other names further down the list include Travis Scott at 16%, Harry Styles at 15%, Daft Punk at 13%, BTS at 7%, and Justin Bieber at 5%.

Trading volume across Coachella-related markets climbed within hours of the rumors spreading on social media. The market is scheduled to remain open until January 31, 2027.

The reaction to the Coachella 2027 lineup is a prime example of how prediction markets react to mainstream entertainment news long before official announcements drop. Traders aggregate information from media reports, social media leaks, and industry chatter to price in probabilities.

While these odds do not represent guaranteed confirmation from the promoter Goldenvoice, they offer a direct view into market consensus—one that shifts dynamically as fresh details emerge.

One of the world’s biggest music festivals is no stranger to blockchain. In 2022, Coachella auctioned ten lifetime-pass NFTs. The collection raised roughly $1.4 million. After FTX’s collapse that November, many holders lost access to their keys, as assets kept on the exchange became stuck.

In 2024, the festival tried again with OpenSea on Avalanche, reissuing NFTs to affected buyers, though demand for the first new collection was initially weak.

#5204. Critical Ethereum Security Alert Issued Over Compromised Software

Blockchain security provider SlowMist has issued an urgent security alert for Ethereum ecosystem users after a complex supply chain attack was detected on Ethersphere's software.

In a recent post on the X social media platform, SlowMist announced that two pieces of software have been compromised by hackers.

The cybersecurity firm claims that attackers were able to break into GitHub accounts and take advantage of legitimate software release processes to deploy malicious software.

The attack could potentially impact users on multiple operating systems, including Windows, macOS and Linux.

The affected software is part of Swarm, a decentralized storage network belonging to the wider Ethereum ecosystem.

Security experts have cautioned that these malicious software releases could enable attackers to compromise cryptocurrency wallet credentials, steal private keys and open up permanent backdoors on affected systems.

One of the most concerning features of this attack is the involvement of Ethereum's blockchain infrastructure in helping manage the malicious software.

SlowMist explains that this malware retrieves and runs malicious JavaScript code.

However, this attack is not just a simple malware infection.

The cybersecurity firm disclosed that attackers are leveraging an Ethereum smart contract as a decentralized command-and-control (C2) configuration repository.

This gives attackers the ability to dynamically update server addresses managing the malware without requiring a new deployment of the malicious software.

Interestingly, the same Ethereum smart contract was previously associated with the infamous SleepyDuck and EtherDuck malware attacks. These campaigns targeted developers using malicious extensions on the Open VSX marketplace.

This points to the possibility that the new attack may be part of a broader operation aimed at compromising cryptocurrency developers and users.

Independent security researchers from Socket and SafeDep also discovered malware aimed at stealing Swarm wallet files and deploying persistent remote access tools.

As a result of this discovery, SlowMist advised users to immediately uninstall the affected package and roll back to the clean version 13.2.0.

In the meantime, users of Swarm Desktop v0.56.2 were advised to update to the clean v0.56.3 software release.

#5202. Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

Sony Axing Game Discs Will Have Outsized Impact on Low-Income Gamers, Experts Say

Gamers in low-income areas will be among the hardest hit by Sony’s decision to stop producing physical game discs from 2028, experts told Decrypt, while one African games industry expert arguing the continent stands to lose out badly.

The secondary market has long been a cheap way for gamers to get hold of games. The PlayStation Store does run sales, but buying digitally assumes a decent internet connection, which rural households are less likely to have.

"By abandoning physical discs, some players at the bottom of the economic spectrum lose access to physical distribution through retail and, more importantly, discounted used sales," Joost van Dreunen, professor at the NYU Stern School of Business and video game business author, told Decrypt. "It won't immediately show up in the financials of major game makers, since their focus is now on creating premium experiences for a luxury class of gamers."

It comes as the cost of being a gamer is trending upwards. Sony raised PS5 prices in March, for example, citing "pressures in the global economic landscape." Similarly, the next generation of Xbox is expected to cost up to $1,800, according to Fortune.

A spokesperson for UK advocacy group Gamer's Voice told Decrypt that Sony's move to cut discs will "likely affect people on lower incomes more."

Isa Muhammad, founder of Africa and Middle East-focused games industry platform Bijetic, told Decrypt that the PS5 is already "out of reach for most Africans." He stressed that those who can buy a console still rely on the second-hand games market to afford to play on the consoles.

Well, at least the highest-income Africans could still take advantage of the PlayStation Store discounts, right? Unfortunately not. Muhammad said that the PlayStation Network is only supported in one African nation: South Africa. Many other nations are missing from the PlayStation Network's official list of supported countries.

"A gamer in Nigeria who wants to buy digitally has to set up an account registered in the U.S. or UK, then pay with foreign gift cards or a dollar card," Muhammad said.

Download speeds in rural, remote, and low-income areas are another major hurdle.

"Bandwidth is now part of what a game costs," van Dreunen said. "Roughly one in four rural [American] households has no wired provider offering 100/20 Mbps, and at 10 Mbps a 50GB download takes about 11 hours."

In Africa and the Middle East, Muhammad said, internet speeds vary significantly across the region—from super-fast connections in Dubai to unreliable ones in Iran. Internet access is also costly in some countries.

"Just recently I paid 75,000 NGN (around $55) for a 450GB broadband bundle, which is more than Nigeria's minimum monthly wage," Muhammad told Decrypt. With GTA 6's pre-load file expected to be 212GB, "a gamer here would first spend $80 on the game, then anywhere from $25 to over $60 just to download it, depending on how they buy data."

And at Nigeria's median download speed of 36.77 Mbps, per Speedtest by Ookla, the download would take nearly 13 hours. Not to mention the fact that they'd need to have a non-Nigerian PlayStation account, a download code corresponding to the country their account is supposedly from, and a way to pay in the local currency.

"Sony's own terms say you must register your account in the country where you live, and Sony can suspend or close accounts created with false information," Muhammad told Decrypt. "So every Nigerian or Egyptian on a US or UK PSN account is breaking the rules from day one."

African gamers live in fear of needing help from Sony and being refused it (or worse, banned) because their accounts break its rules, Muhammad explained. In a future where every purchase is digital, it could open the door to gamers losing accounts that hold every game they've ever purchased.

"If a player in Africa buys GTA 6 and can't recover their account, they may have no choice but to buy it again on a new one, along with everything else they've lost," he said. "A disc-free future will see this become the reality for every new PlayStation game, for gamers across most of Africa."

Sony and PlayStation did not respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

#5201. Bitcoin (BTC) Capitulation: Retail Holders Are Melting, Dropping BTC Off

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin has been losing wallets on the network since October 16 of this year. With the number of non-empty wallets peaking at 59.99 million that day, the network has already lost roughly 108,700 of them as the price dropped back below $75,000. Smaller holders are clearly running out of patience.

Bitcoin's last bullish hope was a short recovery rally towards the $80,000–$81,000 price threshold in early November. It failed to hold on to that momentum and quickly tumbled towards the $76,000 area, which opened the way towards more losses. Since its October top near $87,000, BTC lost roughly 14% of its value and lost everything it built up since late August.

BTC moves below all major moving averages, with most of them aiming downwards and hinting at the aggravation of the current trend on the market. RSI confirms the thesis, moving around 40 and suggesting a bearish momentum, or a sideways movement at least.

Fueled by the falling price, retail is still fearful: smaller traders are merging or emptying their wallets. The network now holds around 59.88 million non-empty wallets, while exchange supply is down near 1.28 million BTC. The shrinking wallet count, however, isn't something that automatically means Bitcoin is being used less.

But where would BTC finally settle down? Realistically, the closest 'fair' support is the $74,000–$75,000 area, where buyers already stepped in this week. Discussing a breakdown below makes little sense, unfortunately, due to the volatile nature of the market.

But even if BTC holds the $74,000 support, bulls might still stay sidelined. There are little to no signs of retail coming back that can help BTC reach key market levels. Even worse, the market in general isn't favoring bulls, so bracing yourself for a prolonged correction might be the wise choice.

In case bulls somehow wake up, a recovery towards $78,000–$80,000 is the main priority, as it's the area where several moving averages are sitting right now. A falling wallet count with the price holding often acts as a sign of sellers getting exhausted, as smaller investors tend to give up when fear is at its highest.

Whether buyers can defend the $74,000 level or not is the main test for the asset right now. The wallet count keeps shrinking, the price is stuck below the moving averages and a short-lived price bounce here won't change the situation as a whole, unfortunately.

Home » Blitz news » XRP Price Tests Support After Sharp Slide From $1.32

XRP hits the same structural support pattern tested numerous times before: here’s where the bounce could take us.

XRP has fallen back to roughly $1.16 after failing to hold gains near $1.32 earlier in the week, putting a closely watched support zone in focus.

The token briefly traded above $1.30 amid signs of growing large-holder balances, but the recovery faded as broader selling pressure returned.

The move leaves XRP price near the lower end of a descending channel that has shaped its price action since mid-October.

Several market watchers have identified $1.14 as the immediate line buyers need to defend; a sustained break below it could extend the recent decline.

On four-hour charts, XRP has repeatedly moved between lower support and resistance near $1.30. A hold around $1.14 could open the way for another attempt at the channel’s upper boundary, implying a rebound of about 13% from recent levels.

XRP LOOKS READY TO BOUNCE$XRP has reached the lower boundary of the descending channel that has defined its price action over the past month.If $1.14 holds as support, XRP could rebound toward the channel’s upper boundary at $1.30.That would represent a potential 13% move… https://t.co/HTMbkwJNQJ pic.twitter.com/lUwUT1bQWq

That outcome remains conditional. XRP slipped below $1.20 during the latest pullback, leaving both daily and weekly chart structures under pressure. Resistance is seen first around $1.30, followed by the $1.35 to $1.40 area that capped the earlier advance.

Ripple’s chief technology officer, David Schwartz, addressed criticism tied to the token’s performance as XRP traded near $1.16. He said he has limited influence over its market price, underscoring the distance between Ripple’s corporate developments and day-to-day moves in a publicly traded asset.

Large-wallet accumulation has been one of the more constructive data points. Increasing whale holdings and limited movement among some major XRP addresses is a pattern that some traders interpret as a sign that longer-term holders are not rushing to sell.

But crypto wallet data does not settle the question of whether the rebound can last. Exchange supply, fresh demand and the source of buying matter more than a single technical level, particularly after XRP’s steep retreat from late-September highs.

#5199. Bitcoin – How BTC could turn its recent price crash into an $87K rally

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Is Bitcoin’s [BTC] growing negative correlation to other risk assets becoming a key headwind?

It has been less than 48 hours since the most recent crypto wipeout resulted in over $1 billion in liquidations, pushing Bitcoin close to $70k, and sending market sentiment into extreme fear. While Bitcoin continues to trade below $75k, the S&P500 has risen by over 2%, pointing to stronger resilience despite the ongoing FUD.

This growing disparity raises questions about Bitcoin’s performance as a macro hedge this cycle. Notably, Peter Schiff shares his view. As his post below highlights, the long-time Bitcoin critic has warned of a deeper BTC correction, citing its underperformance compared to other risk assets. Even precious metals such as gold and silver have gained over 2%, further widening the performance gap.

Against this backdrop, positioning for further downside is starting to make more sense.

According to CoinGlass data, Bitcoin’s long/short ratio is showing bearish signs as it fell back from a 3-month high of 1.4 on the 7th of November, indicating traders are returning to shorting their positions, signaling weaker confidence in BTC’s near-term recovery. The key takeaway? There is no downside liquidity left to be hunted. The recent $1 billion liquidation event has wiped out almost all long-side liquidity.

This can be considered a great chance for bulls to recover. If Bitcoin remains above $75k and accumulation continues, then both early and late short positions could come under pressure. In fact, one analyst highlighted over $11 billion in short liquidity clustered around $87k.

Interestingly, bulls already seem to be positioning for this potential move.

Despite the scale of the recent wipeout, market sentiment is already showing signs of recovery.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index shows that sentiment has improved by four points to 32. This indicates that selling pressure may be losing steam and that confidence is returning. Notably, institutional activity is also adding to this shift. As on-chain data reported, MSTR recently bought an additional 1,036 BTC.

To add to this, MARA Holdings has announced a Q3 net profit of $634.4 million, up from $123.1 million in the previous year. This is backed up by Santiment data showing a distinct increase in accumulation between retail and mid-sized holders. In the past two weeks the market has seen an increase of 1-10 BTC wallets by 10,076 BTC and 10-100 BTC wallets by 4,131 BTC.

In short, just because BTC has failed to outperform other assets does not mean that the rally is dying.

Despite macro uncertainty and bearish positioning being a key factor, there are clear signs of increasing institutional exposure and accumulation across wallet classes. If Bitcoin can hold above $75k, the current divergence could turn into a potential bear trap, with the $87k short-liquidity cluster becoming the next major upside target.

Bitcoin is lagging traditional markets, but institutional buying and whale accumulation remain strong.

If Bitcoin holds $75k, a short squeeze could push prices toward $87k.

#5198. Binance CFO leaves the company to tokenize real assets

Binance CFO Kaiser Ng is leaving after five years to build his own venture, naming Justin Yek as his successor and eyeing tokenization projects.

Binance chief financial officer Kaiser Ng said on Nov. 8 that he had decided to build something of his own after five years in the role, naming Justin Yek as the exchange’s next CFO.

According to XRPScan, XRP Ledger payments volume rises again, reaching 1.16 billion XRP on November 9.

XRP's payment volume is up 207.4% over 24 hours, sitting far below its all-time high of 7 billion. Payments volume growth is substantial, but not record-breaking by any means, with the 30-day average at 580.6 million XRP.

Payments volume rises, but so does the bear market pressure, with a possibility of a further downturn that can make things worse than they already are.

#5196. Ethereum – Will ETH’s $432M liquidation shock put $2K support at risk?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 8th and 9th of November, Ethereum [ETH] witnessed a combined liquidation of $432.34 million worth of leveraged positions. As a result, the ETH price slid from $2,236 to $2,060.

This was the largest wave of liquidations for Ethereum since the 19th of August, when $1.02 billion worth of short positions were taken out in a single day.

At the time of writing, Ethereum was trading just above $2.1k. Swing traders and investors would be keenly watching the $2,000 psychological level to assess if the next wave of selling was upon us.

In other news, Vitalik Buterin presented reasons why Ethereum as a store of value made “more sense to focus on” than ETH as a fee-burning token.

The OI was at $5.236 billion, compared to the 30-day average of $5.850 billion, with a standard deviation of $213.77 million. This meant that the current OI was about 2.87 standard deviations below the 30-day mean.

Meanwhile, persistent selling pressure has pushed the leading altcoin’s price down towards $2,100. Declining OI and downward price pressure could be due to the closure of existing positions, through liquidations or manual exits.

It is not necessarily a sign that there were sizable short positions opened, the analyst wrote.

The ETH price structure was bearish after the loss of the swing point at $2,358. The $2,400 area has since been retested as a resistance zone, and the price has reacted bearishly from there in November.

The OBV was relatively flat over the past ten days, but it has been declining slowly since early October. The MACD was also declining and signaled strong bearish momentum at the time of writing.

The $2,000 round-number support was one level to watch. The $1,840-$1,950 area demand zone where ETH consolidated in July and August was also a place investors can wait for a bullish reaction from.

#5195. XRP Ledger Testnet and Devnet Face Major November Reset Wiping All Data

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP ledger developers have been notified regarding upcoming resets for Testnet and Devnets. According to a statement, XRPL testnet and devnet are scheduled for a reset on November 25, 2026, to improve full history server stability.

Testnet and Devnet for XRP Ledger developers will be reset on Nov 25, 2026 at 5–6 PM PT. All on-chain state (accounts, tokens, AMMs, etc.) will be deleted and will need a re-fund and re-setup afterward. Other networks will continue to operate as usual, including Mainnet, Xahau, and the Hooks Testnet.

Ripple runs Testnet and Devnet, so the community can test out new features and integrations without risking real money.

The XRPL Testnet used to depend on a single full history server, which went down and cannot be recovered without a reset. To avoid this from happening again, an upgrade was performed to increase the server storage capacity.

The resets will delete all ledger data in Testnet and Devnet, including all accounts, transactions, balances, settings, offers, AMMs, escrows, and other data. Testnet will have its Amendment statuses restored to match Mainnet's. However, any AMMs that are re-created after the reset will generally have different account addresses. As a best practice, users are urged not to use the same addresses or key pairs on Mainnet and any test networks.

The important URLs, Network ID, and validator settings for both the devnet and testnets will stay the same. For XRPL users who run an xrpld server that is connected to testnet or Devnet, after the reset they should delete their database data and restart the server.

The last time the XRPL devnet saw a reset was on March 3, 2026. This was necessitated due to the Batch amendment, which required more development at the time and to prevent validators from becoming amendment-blocked, a Devnet reset was required.

The XRPL foundation has launched the XRP Ledger Education Month with up to $40,000 in awards for educators and content creators who explain upcoming amendments like the Lending Protocol or teach how the XRP Ledger works, the role of XRP and the core protocol philosophies.

Since XRPL 3.3.0, memory consumption has been reduced by up to 23% on nodes. Memory growth in XRPL nodes was observed in late summer 2025 and a fix shipped in xrpld 3.3.0.
